<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that two-way nondeterministic finite automata cannot be complemented with a polynomial number of states independent of the alphabet. For each <i>n</i> ≥ 4 we construct an <i>n</i>-state automaton over a finite alphabet whose complement requires at least $`\tfrac12 2^{\lfloor(n-4)/127\rfloor}-1`$ states.

- **C1** (bound, [source](#stmt-main-theorem)): For every integer $n\ge4$, set $H=\{1,\ldots,n-2\}$ and $\Sigma_n=\mathcal R_H$. There is an $n$-state 2NFA $A_n$ recognizing $L_H$ such that every 2NFA recognizing $\Sigma_n^*\setminus L(A_n)$ has at least $$\frac12\,2^{\lfloor(n-4)/127\rfloor}-1$$ states. In particular, there is no polynomial state bound for complementation that is independent of the finite alphabet.
  - Explanation: Alphabet-uniform complementation of two-way nondeterministic automata requires exponentially many states.
- **C2** (bound, [source](#stmt-amp-relation-bound)): **Theorem 2** (Order-reversing image bound). * Let $m\geq1$, let $H$ be a finite set of cardinality $h\geq2$, and let $S_0$ be a submonoid of $\mathcal T_m$. Suppose that a surjective multiplicative map $\phi_0:S_0\to\mathcal R_H$ satisfies $$z\sqsubseteq w\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \phi_0(w)\subseteq\phi_0(z) \qquad(z,w\in S_0). \label{amp:reverse}$$ Then $$2m\geq 2^{\lfloor(h-2)/127\rfloor}. \label{amp:bound}$$ The map need not initially be assumed to preserve identities.*
  - Explanation: An inclusion-reversing surjective multiplicative relation image forces an exponential diagram degree.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-auto-representation)): Let $B$ be an $s$-state 2NFA over a finite alphabet $\Sigma$ in the model fixed in the introduction. There are a monoid homomorphism $$\tau:\Sigma^*\longrightarrow\mathcal T_{s+1},$$ two fixed endmarker diagrams $\lambda,\rho\in\mathcal T_{s+1}$, and fixed positive labels $a,b$ such that, for every word $w$, $$\label{auto:recognition} w\in L(B) \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad (a,b)\in F_{\lambda\tau(w)\rho}.$$ Consequently, if $\tau(u)\sqsubseteq\tau(v)$, then $$\label{auto:context-monotonicity} \alpha u\beta\in L(B) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \alpha v\beta\in L(B) \qquad(\alpha,\beta\in\Sigma^*).$$
  - Explanation: Finite accepting computations are represented by a designated through edge, including stay moves and repeated crossings.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-auto-order-reversal)): Let $H$ be a finite set with at least two elements. If an $s$-state 2NFA recognizes $\Sigma_H^*\setminus L_H$, then a submonoid of $\mathcal T_{s+1}$ admits a surjective unital homomorphism $\phi$ onto $\mathcal R_H$ satisfying $$\label{auto:reverse-order} z\sqsubseteq z' \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \phi(z')\subseteq\phi(z).$$
  - Explanation: Singleton relation contexts induce a well-defined inclusion-reversing relation image from a complement recognizer.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-diag-missing)): Let $e\in\mathcal T_m$ be idempotent. For $z,w\in e\mathcal T_me$, $$\mathcal M_e(zw)\subseteq\mathcal M_e(z)\cup\mathcal M_e(w), \qquad \mathcal M_e(e)=\varnothing.$$ In particular, $\mathcal M_e(z^k)\subseteq\mathcal M_e(z)$ for every positive integer $k$.
  - Explanation: Missing recurrent classes are controlled by the union of the factors’ missing sets within the same corner.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-diag-rectangles)): Let $e\in\mathcal T_m$ be idempotent and let $z\in e\mathcal T_me$. For a positive recurrent class $C\in\mathcal C_e$ and any $p\in C$, set $$A_C=\{u:u(F_eK_e)p\},\qquad B_C=\{v:p(K_eF_e)v\}.$$ The rectangle $A_C\times B_C$ is independent of $p$ and is contained in $F_e$. These rectangles cover $F_e$. If $C\notin\mathcal M_e(z)$, its rectangle is contained in $F_z$. The reflected assertions hold for negative classes and $B_e$. In particular, $$\label{diag:detect} \mathcal M_e(z)=\varnothing\quad\Longrightarrow\quad e\sqsubseteq z.$$
  - Explanation: Recurrent-class rectangles cover through edges; preserving every recurrent class preserves the identity diagram’s edges.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-diag-transport)): Let $e,d\in\mathcal T_m$ be idempotents and let $u,v\in\mathcal T_m$ satisfy $uev=d$. For every $J\subseteq\mathcal C_e$ there exists a single set $J'\subseteq\mathcal C_d$, with $\|J'\|\le2\|J\|$, such that $$\mathcal M_d(uzv)\subseteq J'$$ for every $z\in e\mathcal T_me$ satisfying $\mathcal M_e(z)\subseteq J$ and $uzv\in d\mathcal T_md$.
  - Explanation: A fixed diagram context transports a common missing-set budget to a common target budget.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nest-absorption)): Let $d,e\in\mathcal T_m$ be idempotents with $d\trianglelefteq e$. For either sign $\sigma$, $$K_e^\sigma\subseteq K_d^\sigma, \qquad P_e^\sigma P_d^\sigma\subseteq P_d^\sigma, \qquad P_d^\sigma P_e^\sigma\subseteq P_d^\sigma.$$ In the positive sign one also has $$\label{nest:through-absorption} F_eP_d^+\subseteq P_d^+, \qquad P_d^+F_e\subseteq P_d^+.$$ The corresponding negative-sign inclusions use $B_e$.
  - Explanation: Nested idempotents satisfy the return-closure and recurrent-relation absorption inclusions.
- **C9** (classification, [source](#stmt-nest-containment)): Let $d,e\in\mathcal T_m$ be idempotents with $d\trianglelefteq e$. Every class in $\mathcal C_d$ contains either* 1. *a whole class in $\mathcal C_e\setminus\mathcal M_e(d)$; or* 2. *two distinct whole classes in $\mathcal C_e$.* *All classes in either containment have the same sign.
  - Explanation: Each nested recurrent class contains a surviving old class or distinct old classes of the same sign.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-nest-chain)): Let $b_0,b_1,\ldots,b_k\in\mathcal T_m$ be idempotents such that $b_j\trianglelefteq b_{j-1}$ for $1\leq j\leq k$. Suppose a single set $J_0\subseteq\mathcal C_{b_0}$ satisfies $$\mathcal M_{b_0}(b_j)\subseteq J_0 \qquad(0\leq j\leq k).$$ Then $$\label{nest:chain-bound} \sum_{j=1}^k\bigl\|\mathcal M_{b_{j-1}}(b_j)\bigr\| \leq 2\|J_0\|.$$
  - Explanation: A common initial budget bounds the cumulative missing-class loss along a nested chain.
- **C11** (construction, [source](#stmt-amp-minimal-corner)): Let $A$ be a finite semigroup, let $M$ be a monoid, and let $\theta:A\to M$ be a surjective multiplicative map. For every idempotent $q\in M$, there is an idempotent $d\in A$ with $\theta(d)=q$ that is minimal among such idempotents under $\trianglelefteq$. For every such minimal $d$, restriction of $\theta$ gives a unital surjection $$dAd\longrightarrow qMq.$$ Every element of $dAd$ whose image is a unit of $qMq$ is itself a unit of $dAd$. In particular, every unit of $qMq$ has a unit lift.
  - Explanation: Minimal idempotent lifts give surjective unital corner maps and lifts of units.
- **C12** (construction, [source](#stmt-amp-relation-corner)): Let $P\in\mathcal R_H$ be idempotent, let $F\subseteq H$, and put $i=I_F$. Suppose that $iPi=i$, and define $Q=PiP$. Then $Q$ is idempotent, $Q\trianglelefteq P$, and restriction is a unital monoid isomorphism $$\rho:Q\mathcal R_HQ\longrightarrow\mathcal R_F, \qquad \rho(Z)=iZi. \label{amp:restriction}$$ Its inverse sends $A\in\mathcal R_F$ to $PAP$.
  - Explanation: Restriction identifies a suitable relation corner with the full relation monoid on retained points.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-amp-single-edge)): Let $m\geq1$, let $e\in\mathcal T_m$ be idempotent, and let $S\subseteq e\mathcal T_me$ be a submonoid with identity $e$. Let $H$ be a finite set of cardinality $h\geq2$. Suppose that $\phi:S\to\mathcal R_H$ is a unital surjective homomorphism such that $$z\sqsubseteq w\ \Longrightarrow\ \phi(w)\subseteq\phi(z) \qquad(z,w\in S),$$ and suppose that each permutation of $H$ has a lift that is a unit of $S$. If $a\in S$ is idempotent and, for distinct $x,y\in H$, $$\phi(a)=I_H\cup\{(x,y)\},$$ then $\|\mathcal M_e(a)\|\geq D(h)$.
  - Explanation: Conjugate amplification and restriction force exponential missing-class loss for an added off-diagonal pair.
- **C14** (construction, [source](#stmt-auto-source)): The language $L_H$ is recognized by a 2NFA with exactly $h+2$ states.
  - Explanation: A source automaton guesses a path through the input relations and accepts precisely nonempty products.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-auto-determinization)): There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $n_0$ such that, for every $n\ge n_0$, every 2DFA recognizing the language of the $n$-state 2NFA $A_n$ in Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a> has at least $$c\,2^{\lfloor(n-4)/127\rfloor}$$ states. Hence no polynomial state bound independent of the finite alphabet can determinize all 2NFAs.
  - Explanation: Linear-overhead deterministic complementation transfers the complement lower bound to deterministic simulation.

There are a monoid homomorphism $$\\tau:\\Sigma^*\\longrightarrow\\mathcal T_{s+1},$$ two fixed endmarker diagrams $\\lambda,\\rho\\in\\mathcal T_{s+1}$, and fixed positive labels $a,b$ such that, for every word $w$, $$\\label{auto:recognition}\n  w\\in L(B)\n  \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n  (a,b)\\in F_{\\lambda\\tau(w)\\rho}.$$ Consequently, if $\\tau(u)\\sqsubseteq\\tau(v)$, then $$\\label{auto:context-monotonicity}\n  \\alpha u\\beta\\in L(B)\n  \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n  \\alpha v\\beta\\in L(B)\n  \\qquad(\\alpha,\\beta\\in\\Sigma^*).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-129.1"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-diag-rectangles",
        "exact_quote": "Let $e\\in\\mathcal T_m$ be idempotent and let $z\\in e\\mathcal T_me$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-amp-minimal-corner",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a finite semigroup, let $M$ be a monoid, and let $\\theta:A\\to M$ be a surjective multiplicative map. For every idempotent $q\\in M$, there is an idempotent $d\\in A$ with $\\theta(d)=q$ that is minimal among such idempotents under $\\trianglelefteq$. For every such minimal $d$, restriction of $\\theta$ gives a unital surjection $$dAd\\longrightarrow qMq.$$ Every element of $dAd$ whose image is a unit of $qMq$ is itself a unit of $dAd$. In particular, every unit of $qMq$ has a unit lift.",
        "source_id": "PM-129.1"
      },

In particular, every unit of $qMq$ has a unit lift.",
          "source_id": "PM-129.1"
        }

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Two-way nondeterministic finite automaton** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A two-way nondeterministic finite automaton (2NFA) has a finite set of states and a single read-only head, which may move in either direction along an input bounded by endmarkers. It accepts when some finite computation reaches an accepting state.
- **Binary relation monoid** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a finite set $H$, let $\mathcal R_H$ denote all binary relations on $H$. Products are composed in path order: $(p,r)\in AB$ if and only if $(p,q)\in A$ and $(q,r)\in B$ for some $q$.
- **Word relation product** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The product $r(w)$ of a word $w=R_1\cdots R_k$ is $R_1\cdots R_k$, and the empty product is $r(\varepsilon)=I_H=\{(p,p):p\in H\}$.
- **Relation-product liveness language** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Define the relation-product liveness language $$L_H=\{w\in\Sigma_H^*:r(w)\ne\varnothing\}.$$
- **Path diagram** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Fix disjoint sets $D^+$ and $D^-$, each of size $m\ge1$. A diagram $a$ consists of four arbitrary relations $$F_a\subseteq D^+\times D^+,\quad B_a\subseteq D^-\times D^-,\quad L_a\subseteq D^+\times D^-,\quad R_a\subseteq D^-\times D^+.$$
- **Reflexive transitive closure** ([source](#diag:star)): On a single set, $A^*$ denotes the reflexive transitive closure, so it includes paths of length zero.
- **Idempotent recurrent relations** ([source](#diag:idempotent)): For an idempotent $e\in\mathcal T_m$, define $$\label{diag:KP} \begin{aligned} K_e^+&=(L_eR_e)^*,& P_e^+&=K_e^+F_eK_e^+,\\ K_e^-&=(R_eL_e)^*,& P_e^-&=K_e^-B_eK_e^-. \end{aligned}$$
- **Recurrent point** ([source](#diag:idempotent)): A point $x\in D^\sigma$ is *recurrent* if $xP_e^\sigma x$.
- **Recurrent classes** ([source](#diag:idempotent)): Mutual relatedness under $P_e^\sigma$ is an equivalence relation on the recurrent points. Let $\mathcal C_e$ be the collection of its classes for both signs, keeping the signs distinct.
- **Diagram corner** ([source](#diag:corner)): The *corner* $e\mathcal T_me=\{eae:a\in\mathcal T_m\}$ is a monoid with identity $e$; membership is equivalent to $ez=ze=z$.
- **Missing set** ([source](#diag:corner)): Hence the following is well-defined: $$\mathcal M_e(z)=\{C\in\mathcal C_e: xU_e^\sigma(z)x\text{ fails for }x\in C, \text{ where }C\subseteq D^\sigma\}.$$ We call $\mathcal M_e(z)$ the *missing set* of $z$ relative to $e$.
- **Nested idempotents** ([source](#nest:section)): For idempotents in a semigroup, write $d\trianglelefteq e$ if $de=ed=d$, and say that $d$ is *nested under* $e$.
- **Unit** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A *unit* means an element with a two-sided inverse relative to the specified monoid identity.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-main-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-auto-conventions): Alphabet-uniform complementation of two-way nondeterministic automata requires exponentially many states. -> Alphabet-uniform complementation of two-way nondeterministic automata requires exponentially many states.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-amp-relation-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-single-edge-2): An inclusion-reversing surjective multiplicative relation image forces an exponential diagram degree. -> An inclusion-reversing surjective multiplicative relation image forces an exponential diagram degree.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-auto-representation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-auto-source-2): Finite accepting computations are represented by a designated through edge, including stay moves and repeated crossings. -> Finite accepting computations are represented by a designated through edge, including stay moves and repeated crossings.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-auto-order-reversal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-auto-order-reversal): Singleton relation contexts induce a well-defined inclusion-reversing relation image from a complement recognizer. -> Singleton relation contexts induce a well-defined inclusion-reversing relation image from a complement recognizer.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-diag-missing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-diag-missing): Missing recurrent classes are controlled by the union of the factors’ missing sets within the same corner. -> Missing recurrent classes are controlled by the union of the factors’ missing sets within the same corner.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-diag-rectangles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-diag-rectangles): Recurrent-class rectangles cover through edges; preserving every recurrent class preserves the identity diagram’s edges. -> Recurrent-class rectangles cover through edges; preserving every recurrent class preserves the identity diagram’s edges.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-diag-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-diag-transport): A fixed diagram context transports a common missing-set budget to a common target budget. -> A fixed diagram context transports a common missing-set budget to a common target budget.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-nest-absorption) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nest-absorption): Nested idempotents satisfy the return-closure and recurrent-relation absorption inclusions. -> Nested idempotents satisfy the return-closure and recurrent-relation absorption inclusions.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-nest-containment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nest-containment): Each nested recurrent class contains a surviving old class or distinct old classes of the same sign. -> Each nested recurrent class contains a surviving old class or distinct old classes of the same sign.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-nest-chain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nest-chain): A common initial budget bounds the cumulative missing-class loss along a nested chain. -> A common initial budget bounds the cumulative missing-class loss along a nested chain.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-amp-minimal-corner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-minimal-corner): Minimal idempotent lifts give surjective unital corner maps and lifts of units. -> Minimal idempotent lifts give surjective unital corner maps and lifts of units.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-amp-relation-corner) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-relation-corner): Restriction identifies a suitable relation corner with the full relation monoid on retained points. -> Restriction identifies a suitable relation corner with the full relation monoid on retained points.
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-amp-single-edge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-amp-single-edge): Conjugate amplification and restriction force exponential missing-class loss for an added off-diagonal pair. -> Conjugate amplification and restriction force exponential missing-class loss for an added off-diagonal pair.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-auto-source) / [proof](#proof-stmt-auto-source): A source automaton guesses a path through the input relations and accepts precisely nonempty products. -> A source automaton guesses a path through the input relations and accepts precisely nonempty products.
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-auto-determinization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-auto-determinization): Linear-overhead deterministic complementation transfers the complement lower bound to deterministic simulation. -> Linear-overhead deterministic complementation transfers the complement lower bound to deterministic simulation.

For a positive recurrent class $C\\in\\mathcal C_e$ and any $p\\in C$, set $$A_C=\\{u:u(F_eK_e)p\\},\\qquad\n B_C=\\{v:p(K_eF_e)v\\}.$$ The rectangle $A_C\\times B_C$ is independent of $p$ and is contained in $F_e$. These rectangles cover $F_e$. If $C\\notin\\mathcal M_e(z)$, its rectangle is contained in $F_z$. The reflected assertions hold for negative classes and $B_e$. In particular, $$\\label{diag:detect}\n \\mathcal M_e(z)=\\varnothing\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad e\\sqsubseteq z.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-129.1"
        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-live**: Acceptance is equivalent to a nonempty product of the input relations.
- **I-empty**: The source automaton accepts the empty word.
- **I-uniform**: The complementation obstruction concerns bounds uniform over finite alphabets.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-fixed**: Claim the same lower bound for one fixed alphabet. -> The growing alphabet is part of the family.
- **A-time**: Infer a time lower bound or a separation of uniform space classes. -> The result compares finite-state machines without a work tape.
- **A-swap**: Complement a looping deterministic machine by exchanging accepting and rejecting states. -> Infinite nonaccepting computations remain a separate obligation.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The source automaton is explicitly constructed by guessing a path through the input relations. This finite computation verifies its acceptance criterion; it does not certify the exponential lower bound.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of cases, each with integer h and a word of relation edge lists.
# PRECONDITIONS: 2 <= h <= 3; word length <= 3; endpoints are integers in range(h).
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of booleans indicating nonempty relation products.
# TIME: O(sum((len(word)+1)*h**3)) exact integer operations.
# SPACE: O(sum(input sizes) + h**2 + number of cases).
# ARITHMETIC: exact arbitrary-precision integers; Booleanize each product entry.
# FAILURE: malformed input or a violated precondition raises an exception.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz
cases = json.load(sys.stdin)
answers = []
for case in cases:
    h, word = case["h"], case["word"]
    if type(h) is not int or not 2 <= h <= 3 or len(word) > 3:
        raise ValueError("unsupported finite domain")
    product = [[fmpz(int(i == j)) for j in range(h)] for i in range(h)]
    for edges in word:
        relation = [[fmpz(0) for _ in range(h)] for _ in range(h)]
        for p, q in edges:
            if type(p) is not int or type(q) is not int or not (0 <= p < h and 0 <= q < h):
                raise ValueError("invalid endpoint")
            relation[p][q] = fmpz(1)
        product = [[fmpz(int(sum(product[i][k] * relation[k][j] for k in range(h)) > 0)) for j in range(h)] for i in range(h)]
    answers.append(any(entry != 0 for row in product for entry in row))
print(json.dumps(answers))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The state lower bound constrains language-complementation and determinization components implemented as read-only finite automata. Applying it to a software component requires matching that computation model and the alphabet-uniform requirement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no game or simulation contract is established. The analyzed computations use a read-only head and finite-state control; the state lower bound supplies no simulation-time claim. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no engineering tool contract is established. The finite automaton construction and diagram representation could be studied by a tool, but the paper does not supply a tool interface or tool performance guarantee. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The source construction gives a finite procedure for recognizing nonempty relation products. Relation multiplication gives an exact way to evaluate the same language on a finite word; the state lower bound concerns complementing finite automata rather than general algorithm running time. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The path diagram represents possible boundary entries and exits using through and return relations. Its inclusion order and composition can support finite computation summaries, provided the first-outer-exit convention and directional label types are preserved. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no accelerator architecture or hardware resource contract is established. The result counts automaton states and explicitly excludes a running-time restriction. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the relation-product liveness language and finite-state lower bound establish no cryptographic primitive, threat model, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no learning procedure or statistical guarantee is established for the finite automata or relation-product language. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no optimization objective, feasible-solution contract, or operations-research algorithm is established. The nested loss budget is a proof device for finite-state lower bounds. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the computation model has a single read-only head and supplies no process, network, consistency, or distributed communication contract. |
| other (Automata state complexity and finite semigroup theory) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The main application is automata state complexity, supported by a finite algebraic obstruction using path-diagram monoids, idempotent corners, and order-reversing relation images. The transport and nested-class bounds may be adapted to other order-reversing images with the same hypotheses. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (counterexample_scope): The complementation lower bound is uniform over finite alphabets and uses a growing alphabet. Blocked use: ['Claiming the same lower bound over one fixed alphabet.']
- **L2** (formal_scope): The parameter counts states rather than the size of the transition table. Blocked use: ['Treating the state lower bound as a transition-table size lower bound.']
- **L3** (formal_scope): The bounds concern finite-state machines without a work tape. Blocked use: ['Claiming a separation between uniform logarithmic-space complexity classes.']
- **L4** (formal_scope): The bounds impose no restriction on input length or on the running time of successful computations. Blocked use: ['Reading the state bound as an input-length or running-time bound.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): Acceptance requires a finite computation reaching an accepting state; nonaccepting infinite computations do not accept. Blocked use: ['Counting an infinite nonaccepting computation as acceptance.']
- **L6** (edge_case): The initial configuration may itself accept, and all initial and accepting states count toward the exact state total. Blocked use: ['Dropping initial or accepting states from the state count.', 'Excluding acceptance in the initial configuration while retaining the exact offsets.']
- **L7** (edge_case): The liveness language contains the empty word when the underlying set is nonempty. Blocked use: ['Rejecting the empty word in the source recognizer.']
- **L8** (counterexample_scope): The obstruction concerns the read-only automaton model; first-visit tape rewriting is an additional resource. Blocked use: ['Applying the obstruction to automata permitted to rewrite a tape cell on its first visit.']
- **L9** (edge_case): Other starting positions and acceptance conventions may change additive state offsets. Blocked use: ['Transferring the exact state offsets unchanged to another automaton convention.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The order-reversing image bound requires a surjective multiplicative map and reversal of inclusion. Blocked use: ['Applying the image bound to an arbitrary map lacking surjectivity, multiplicativity or order reversal.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): Transport controls a family through a single common target missing set. Blocked use: ['Replacing the common-set hypothesis with unrelated individual cardinality bounds.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): Nesting of idempotents differs from inclusion of diagram edges. Blocked use: ['Substituting edge inclusion for nesting in the chain bound.']
- **L13** (edge_case): Complementing a deterministic recognizer with nonaccepting loops requires more than exchanging acceptance and rejection. Blocked use: ['Complementing a potentially looping deterministic machine by exchanging accepting and rejecting states alone.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 129 (`lean/docs/129.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [An exponential state lower bound for two-way nondeterministic complementation](../../preprints/An-exponential-state-lower-bound-for-two-way-nondeterministic-complementation-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [An exponential two-way deterministic state lower bound for one-way liveness](../../preprints/An-exponential-two-way-deterministic-state-lower-bound-for-one-way-liveness-September-25-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The paper asks how many states are needed to complement or determinize two-way nondeterministic finite automata. The formalization gives, for every $n\ge4$, an explicit $n$-state automaton whose complement requires at least $\tfrac12 2^{\lfloor(n-4)/127\rfloor}-1$ states. For the same family of relational languages and every $n\ge131$, every equivalent deterministic two-way automaton requires at least $\tfrac12 2^{\lfloor(n-4)/127\rfloor}$ states.
>
> The alphabet grows with $n$, so neither complementation nor determinization has a polynomial state bound uniform over alphabets. The model has two endmarkers, left, right, and stay moves, and acceptance by a finite run. The separate results about one-way liveness and the Sakoda–Sipser problem are outside this scope.
>
> The formalization gives a family of one-way nondeterministic automata with $h+3$ states whose languages require exponentially many states for two-way deterministic recognition. For every $h\ge2$, a deterministic recognizer with $s$ states satisfies $2^{\lfloor(h-2)/31\rfloor}\le4(s+2)^2$ under both acceptance conventions considered in the paper; one convention has the sharper factor $4(s+1)^2$. The alphabet may grow with $h$, so no alphabet-independent polynomial simulation bound holds.

Comparator source `TwoWayComplementation.lean` (1452 bytes, sha256 `4e47aa239705ac77757073930d4d88e4128e30fba8341284a42c8958a927a6bb`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace TwoWayComplementation

open scoped SetRel

def relStar {α : Type*} (R : SetRel α α) : SetRel α α :=
  {(x, y) | Relation.ReflTransGen (fun a b => (a, b) ∈ R) x y}

structure TwoNFA (Sigma Q : Type*) where
  initial : Q
  accepting : Set Q
  transition : Q → (Fin 2 ⊕ Sigma) → Set (Q × Fin 3)

def tapeSymbol {Sigma : Type*} (w : List Sigma) (p : Fin (w.length + 2)) : Fin 2 ⊕ Sigma :=
  if hleft : p.val = 0 then .inl 0
  else if hright : p.val = w.length + 1 then .inl 1
  else .inr (w[p.val - 1]'(by omega))

namespace TwoNFA

def step {Sigma Q : Type*} (A : TwoNFA Sigma Q) (w : List Sigma) :
    SetRel (Q × Fin (w.length + 2)) (Q × Fin (w.length + 2)) :=
  {(c, d) | ∃ move : Fin 3,
    (d.1, move) ∈ A.transition c.1 (tapeSymbol w c.2) ∧
    (d.2.val : ℤ) = (c.2.val : ℤ) + (move.val : ℤ) - 1}

def language {Sigma Q : Type*} (A : TwoNFA Sigma Q) : Set (List Sigma) :=
  {w | ∃ c, c.1 ∈ A.accepting ∧
    ((A.initial, ⟨0, by omega⟩), c) ∈ relStar (A.step w)}

end TwoNFA

theorem complementation_lower_bound_finite (n : ℕ) (hn : 4 ≤ n) :
    ∃ A : TwoNFA (SetRel (Fin (n - 2)) (Fin (n - 2))) (Fin n),
      ∀ (Q : Type*) [Fintype Q], ∀ B : TwoNFA (SetRel (Fin (n - 2)) (Fin (n - 2))) Q,
        B.language = A.languageᶜ →
          (1 / 2 : ℝ) * 2 ^ ((n - 4) / 127) - 1 ≤ (Fintype.card Q : ℝ) := by
  sorry

end TwoWayComplementation

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that two-way nondeterministic finite automata cannot be complemented with a polynomial number of states independent of the alphabet. For each <i>n</i> ≥ 4 we construct an <i>n</i>-state automaton over a finite alphabet whose complement requires at least $`\tfrac12 2^{\lfloor(n-4)/127\rfloor}-1`$ states.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A two-way nondeterministic finite automaton (2NFA) has a finite set of states and a single read-only head, which may move in either direction along an input bounded by endmarkers. It accepts when some finite computation reaches an accepting state. The complementation problem asks whether every $n$-state 2NFA over a finite alphabet $\Sigma$ has a 2NFA for the complementary language with at most $p(n)$ states, for one polynomial $p$ independent of $\Sigma$. We answer this question negatively.

For exact state counts, there is one initial state and a set of accepting states. Each transition depends only on the current state and scanned symbol, updates the state, and moves the head left, right, or not at all. We start the head on the left endmarker and forbid moves beyond either endmarker. Missing transitions reject that branch; infinite nonaccepting computations do not accept. An accepting initial configuration counts as acceptance. All states, including initial and accepting states, are counted. A deterministic two-way automaton (2DFA) has at most one successor for each state and scanned symbol.

For a finite set $H$, let $\mathcal R_H$ denote all binary relations on $H$. Products are composed in path order: $(p,r)\in AB$ if and only if $(p,q)\in A$ and $(q,r)\in B$ for some $q$. Use $\Sigma_H=\mathcal R_H$ as an input alphabet. The product $r(w)$ of a word $w=R_1\cdots R_k$ is $R_1\cdots R_k$, and the empty product is $r(\varepsilon)=I_H=\{(p,p):p\in H\}$. Define the relation-product liveness language $$L_H=\{w\in\Sigma_H^*:r(w)\ne\varnothing\}.$$ In particular, $L_H$ contains the empty word when $H$ is nonempty. For nonempty words, this is Sakoda and Sipser’s family $B_h$, where $h=|H|$ (Sakoda and Sipser 1978, sec. 2.1 and Theorem 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[SakodaSipser1978](#ref-SakodaSipser1978)]; the name *one-way liveness* is used in (Kapoutsis 2006, sec. 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Kapoutsis2006](#ref-Kapoutsis2006)].

<a id="stmt-main-theorem"></a>
<div id="main:theorem" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every integer $n\ge4$, set $H=\{1,\ldots,n-2\}$ and $\Sigma_n=\mathcal R_H$. There is an $n$-state 2NFA $A_n$ recognizing $L_H$ such that every 2NFA recognizing $\Sigma_n^*\setminus L(A_n)$ has at least $$\frac12\,2^{\lfloor(n-4)/127\rfloor}-1$$ states. In particular, there is no polynomial state bound for complementation that is independent of the finite alphabet.*

</div>

The alphabet $\Sigma_n$ consists of all binary relations on a set of $n-2$ points, so $|\Sigma_n|=2^{(n-2)^2}$. The parameter is the number of states, not the size of the transition table. Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a> concerns bounds uniform over finite alphabets; it does not assert a lower bound over one fixed alphabet.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### History and significance.

Sakoda and Sipser’s work on nondeterminism and two-way finite automata (Sakoda and Sipser 1978) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[SakodaSipser1978](#ref-SakodaSipser1978)] initiated the central state-succinctness problem of simulating two-way nondeterministic automata deterministically. Complementation is a separate question: here the target remains nondeterministic. Vardi’s construction gives an exponential-state one-way nondeterministic automaton for the complement of a two-way nondeterministic automaton (Vardi 1989, Theorem 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Vardi1989](#ref-Vardi1989)]. It certifies nonacceptance by locally consistent sets of states. For sweeping automata, whose head can reverse direction only at the endmarkers, Kapoutsis proved that the complement of one-way liveness requires exponentially many states in every sweeping 2NFA (Kapoutsis 2006, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Kapoutsis2006](#ref-Kapoutsis2006)]. Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a> allows unrestricted two-way motion in the complementing automaton.

For deterministic two-way automata, complementation has a linear state bound independent of the alphabet (Geffert, Mereghetti, and Pighizzini 2007) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[GeffertMereghettiPighizzini2007](#ref-GeffertMereghettiPighizzini2007)]. Guillon, Prigioniero, and Taheri describe polynomial 2NFA complementation as open (Guillon, Prigioniero, and Taheri 2026, sec. 1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[GuillonPrigionieroTaheri2026](#ref-GuillonPrigionieroTaheri2026)] and give polynomial complementation by 1-limited automata (Guillon, Prigioniero, and Taheri 2026, Theorem 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[GuillonPrigionieroTaheri2026](#ref-GuillonPrigionieroTaheri2026)]. These automata may rewrite a tape cell on its first visit; this resource is absent from the read-only model considered here. Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a> resolves the alphabet-uniform polynomial complementation question negatively. The same growing-alphabet family also requires exponentially many states in every equivalent deterministic two-way automaton: a smaller deterministic simulator could be complemented with linear state overhead. Corollary <a href="#auto:determinization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="auto:determinization">16</a> makes this implication explicit, including the treatment of infinite nonaccepting computations.

The companion paper (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CompanionDeterminization2026](#ref-CompanionDeterminization2026)] proves a deterministic lower bound with a larger exponential rate for the same language, by an independent matching-diagram argument. We compare the exact bounds after Corollary <a href="#auto:determinization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="auto:determinization">16</a>. The new obstruction here applies even when the complementing machine is nondeterministic.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### The argument.

An automaton with $h+2$ states guesses a path through the relations and accepts exactly when their product is nonempty. We prove that recognizing product emptiness requires exponentially many states.

The proof separates a representation of computations from a finite algebraic lower bound. A segment of a computation is represented by four relations recording the possible entries and exits through its two boundaries. These *path diagrams* compose by joining boundaries. Their monoid is the monoid of partitioned binary relations introduced by Martin and Mazorchuk (Martin and Mazorchuk 2013, secs. 2.1–2.3) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[MartinMazorchuk2013](#ref-MartinMazorchuk2013)], written with separate labels for the two directions of travel. Adding edges only increases the available accepting paths. On the other hand, singleton relation contexts test the absence of any prescribed pair. Consequently a machine for product emptiness induces a surjective multiplicative map from its path diagrams to the relation monoid that reverses inclusion.

To bound such maps, we attach recurrent classes of boundary labels to an idempotent diagram $e$, meaning $e^2=e$. These classes group labels joined in both directions by through paths together with returns. Their precise definition is given in Section <a href="#sec:diagrams" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:diagrams">2</a>. The diagrams $z$ satisfying $ez=ze=z$ form a submonoid with identity $e$, called its *corner*. Such a $z$ may destroy the loops on some of those classes; we record the destroyed classes as a missing set. Two structural facts control this loss. First, one common missing-set bound transports through any fixed diagram context with only a factor of two. Second, along a chain of nested idempotents the sum of successive losses is bounded by twice a single initial missing-set budget. The second fact permits the corner identity to change during the argument.

For an integer parameter $t$, the amplification step converts $2t$ conjugates of one relation with an added off-diagonal pair into $t^2$ additions sharing a common budget. Each addition, after restriction to a smaller relation corner, incurs an inductively bounded loss. Taking $t=64$ gives the exponential recurrence. This strategy adapts the conjugate-amplification pattern of the matching-diagram rank-loss argument in (OpenAI 2026, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[CompanionDeterminization2026](#ref-CompanionDeterminization2026)]; that argument concerns a deterministic state lower bound. Here all diagram paths may be nondeterministic, and the transport and nested-class bounds are proved anew. They are stated independently of automata and may be useful for other order-reversing images of finite path monoids.

Section <a href="#sec:diagrams" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:diagrams">2</a> states the algebraic bound and derives the order-reversing map before developing recurrent classes and transport. Section <a href="#nest:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nest:section">3</a> proves the chain bound, and Section <a href="#amp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="amp:section">4</a> proves the algebraic lower bound. Section <a href="#auto:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="auto:section">5</a> proves the promised automata representation and completes the proof of Theorem <a href="#main:theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="main:theorem">1</a>.

The bounds impose no restriction on input length or on the running time of a successful computation. They compare finite-state machines without a work tape, and give no separation between uniform logarithmic-space complexity classes.

<a id="sec:diagrams"></a>
### Path diagrams and missing recurrent classes

We first describe finite paths through a segment, without assuming that the paths come from an automaton. Our goal is a loss measure that detects edge inclusion and remains controlled when a segment is put in context.

<a id="diag:star"></a>
Relations are composed in *path order*: $x(AB)y$ means that $xAz$ and $zBy$ for some $z$. On a single set, $A^*$ denotes the reflexive transitive closure, so it includes paths of length zero. We use the elementary path identities $$\label{diag:star}
 (A\cup B)^*=A^*(BA^*)^*,\qquad
 A(BA)^*=(AB)^*A.$$ The first groups successive $A$-steps between $B$-steps; the second groups the same alternating path from its opposite end. These identities hold whenever the relation types permit the displayed products.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### The diagram monoid

Fix disjoint sets $D^+$ and $D^-$, each of size $m\ge1$. A diagram $a$ consists of four arbitrary relations $$F_a\subseteq D^+\times D^+,\quad
 B_a\subseteq D^-\times D^-,\quad
 L_a\subseteq D^+\times D^-,\quad
 R_a\subseteq D^-\times D^+.$$ Entering the segment on the left uses a label of $D^+$; $F_a$ records an exit on the right and $L_a$ an exit on the left. Entering on the right uses a label of $D^-$; $B_a$ records an exit on the left and $R_a$ an exit on the right. The relations $F_a,B_a$ are the *through relations*, and $L_a,R_a$ are the *return relations*; see Figure <a href="#fig:diagram" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:diagram">1</a>.

<figure id="fig:diagram">

<figcaption>The four relations of a path diagram. Each arrow represents a relation between entire label sets, not a single deterministic edge. In a product, an exit enters the neighboring segment with the same label.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-diagram"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:diagram`)

 \draw[->] (lp) to[bend right=35] node[left] {$L_a$}(lm);
 \draw[->] (rm) to[bend right=35] node[right] {$R_a$}(rp);
